Yeah, no, even in a cage at a pet store, if I saw a mouse, I'd have that same panic. My heart would go crazy and I'd just need to run.\n\nDoctor: Would your reaction be any different if you saw a mouse compared to seeing a rat?\n\nPatient: No, it wouldn't make a difference at all. A mouse, a rat... it's the exact same panic for me. Either one of them just sets me off completely. \n\nMy sister, though, she thinks mice are actually cute. Can you imagine? She used to read those Beatrix Potter books to her kids—you know, the ones with the little mice in the bonnets and aprons? *The Tale of Two Bad Mice*, I think one was called. They had these gorgeous illustrations. My sister always loved watercolor paintings. She actually took a watercolor class at the community center a few years ago. She painted this lovely picture of a lighthouse for my mother's birthday. \n\nMy mother loved lighthouses. She collected these little ceramic ones. She had a whole curio cabinet full of them in the dining room. Every time we went on vacation, we had to find a gift shop so she could buy a new one. We went to Cape Cod once when I was a teenager, and I swear we spent more time looking at ceramic lighthouses than we did at the actual ocean. The water there was freezing anyway. We had clam chowder in bread bowls, though, which was nice. I tried making clam chowder at home once, but it just didn't taste the same. I think I didn't use enough cream, or maybe it was the potatoes... I always buy the red potatoes, but maybe I should have used Russets. Russets are better for baking, I know that. I like a baked potato with just a little bit of butter and chives. My husband puts sour cream and bacon bits on his, but that's just too heavy for me, especially before bed.\n\nBut, um, yeah. A mouse or a rat, it doesn't matter. The size doesn't change anything for me. It's the same terrible feeling either way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your fear of rats and mice is worse when you're at work or is it about the same everywhere?\n\nPatient: Well, I'd say it's about the same everywhere, honestly. It doesn't really matter if I'm at work or at home. I clean buildings for a living, mostly those big corporate offices downtown, you know, the ones with the massive glass lobbies. We have to use a special squeegee for those windows so they don't streak. My supervisor, Gary, he is an absolute stickler about streaks. He used to manage a car wash before he came to our cleaning company, so I guess he's just used to staring at glass all day. \n\nHe's a nice guy, though. He brought in donuts last Friday because it was his daughter's birthday. She just turned sixteen, so she's getting her driver's license. I remember when my kids were learning to drive. My oldest, he backed right into our mailbox on his very first try. Just completely flattened it. We had to go down to the hardware store and buy a new wooden post and a new metal box and everything. The guy at the hardware store was so helpful, he even told us exactly how to mix the concrete to set the post in the ground. You have to get the water ratio just right or it won't cure properly. \n\nI guess it's a lot like baking, really. If you put too much flour in a cake, it gets all dry and crumbly. I made a lemon pound cake for Easter last year that turned out a little too dry, but I whipped up a nice sugar glaze for it, so nobody really complained. The secret to a good glaze is just a tiny splash of vanilla extract along with the lemon juice. It just cuts the tartness perfectly. I buy the real vanilla, too, not that imitation stuff. It costs a little more, but it's worth it...\n\nBut, um, anyway... no, it's not worse at work. It's just as bad everywhere. If I see one of those things, I panic, no matter what building I'm in.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of rats and mice would be less intense if you were to change jobs to something that doesn't involve cleaning or being in areas where rats and mice are more likely to be found?\n\nPatient: Well, changing jobs... I don't know if the fear itself would be less intense, but it would definitely make my day-to-day life a lot easier. Right now, it's really starting to mess with my work. I've actually had to refuse or put off cleaning certain buildings because I know there's a problem there, or even just if I suspect it. And my supervisor, Gary—the one I was telling you about with the squeegees—he's starting to notice. He's been questioning whether I can be counted on for the regular routes. \n\nIt really worries me because I was hoping to get a lead position soon, or at least some better shifts. The day shift would be so nice. Working nights means I miss all the prime-time television. I have to record everything on the DVR. My husband always forgets how to use the DVR remote, so if I don't set it up before I leave, he just ends up watching reruns of old Westerns on cable. He loves *Gunsmoke*. He's watched every single episode, I swear. He even bought a cowboy hat at a flea market a couple of years ago. He wore it while he was mowing the lawn once, and our neighbor, Mr. Henderson, asked if he was going to a rodeo. Mr. Henderson is a hoot. He grows the most amazing tomatoes in his backyard. He gave us a whole basket of them last summer. They were those big, ugly heirloom ones, but they tasted incredible. I made fresh bruschetta with them, just a little olive oil, basil, and garlic on toasted baguette slices. You really have to rub the raw garlic on the toasted bread to get the best flavor... \n\nBut, um, yeah... if I didn't have to clean those older buildings where they might be, I wouldn't be risking my job or my chances at a promotion. So, a different job might not cure the fear, but it would stop it from ruining my career.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of rats and mice is something that you've always had, or is it something that developed over time after a specific incident?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not something I've always had, no. It really started about two years ago. That's when we moved into this older apartment building across town. It's one of those pre-war buildings, you know, with the thick plaster walls and the radiators that clank all winter long. The radiators are actually really nice because they dry out your wet winter coats so fast. I always hang my heavy wool coat on a chair right next to it. \n\nI bought that coat at a second-hand shop on Main Street. It was such a steal, only twenty dollars, and it has these beautiful tortoiseshell buttons. I love finding bargains like that. My friend Carol and I used to go thrift shopping every Saturday morning. We'd get up early, grab a coffee at the diner, and just hunt for treasures all day. Carol is the one who found this amazing vintage Pyrex bowl set, the one with the primary colors. She uses them for everything now. She makes this incredible potato salad for the Fourth of July block party every year. She puts a little bit of dill pickle relish in it, which I never thought of doing, but it gives it such a nice tang. I usually just use celery and hard-boiled eggs in mine, but maybe I'll try the relish next time... \n\nBut anyway, yeah, when we moved into that apartment building two years ago, people in the building kept talking about rats. Just constantly mentioning them in the laundry room or the hallway. And that's really when this whole terrible fear started for me.\n\nDoctor: Would your fear of rats and mice be less intense if you were to see them in a very controlled and safe environment, such as a laboratory or a wildlife documentary?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't think it would matter if it was in a documentary or a lab or anywhere else, honestly. If I even think they're around, like if I hear scratching near a wall or see something move by a pipe, my whole body just reacts right away. My heart starts pounding, my hands shake, and I just feel like I have to get out of there before I can even think straight. \n\nIt's kind of like that time my sister and I got stuck in an elevator at the mall. We were just trying to get up to the food court because they have this amazing pretzel stand up there. They dip the pretzels in this warm cinnamon butter, and oh, it's just heaven. We usually get one to share and a large lemonade. But anyway, the elevator just stopped between floors. We were in there for forty-five minutes. My sister, she's claustrophobic, so she was hyperventilating the whole time. I had to keep telling her to breathe into a paper bag. I always keep a little paper bag from the pharmacy in my purse, just in case. You never know when you might need one. I actually got a really nice new purse at a department store sale last week. It's a crossbody bag, real leather, in this beautiful deep burgundy color. I've always loved burgundy. It goes with almost everything in my winter wardrobe. I have this gorgeous burgundy scarf that my mother knitted for me before she passed away. She was such a talented knitter. She could knit a whole sweater in just a few days...\n\nBut, um, yeah... no, seeing them on a TV screen or in a cage wouldn't help. The panic just hits me instantly, no matter what.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your fear of rats and mice significantly interferes with your daily life and causes you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Oh, absolutely. It's just... it's exhausting, honestly. I feel like I'm always on edge, just waiting to see something scurry by, and the stress of it is really wearing me down. It takes a lot out of you, being that worried all the time. \n\nKind of like how tired I feel after a big holiday dinner. I always host Thanksgiving at our place, and the preparation is just a nightmare. I start baking pies two days before. I make a pumpkin pie from scratch—well, I use the canned pumpkin, but the crust is from scratch. My mother-in-law gave me her secret crust recipe before she moved to Florida. She lives in a really nice retirement community down there now, right near the beach. They have a clubhouse with a pool and they do water aerobics every Tuesday morning. I went down to visit her last February because the winters up here just get so dreary, you know? The slush on the roads ruins all my good shoes. I had to throw away a perfectly good pair of suede boots last year because the salt stains just wouldn't come out. I tried vinegar, I tried that special brush they sell at the shoe store, but nothing worked. The guy at the shoe store was so rude about it, too. He acted like it was my fault I wore them outside in the snow. Where else am I supposed to wear winter boots? \n\nBut, um... I'm sorry, I lost my train of thought. What were we talking about? Oh, right, the distress. Yes. It really does make my daily life incredibly hard. I just want to go to work and live my life without feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Would your fear of rats and mice be less intense if you were in a situation where you knew for certain that there were no rats or mice present, such as a very clean and modern environment?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't really know... I mean, I clean buildings for a living, right? I'm a janitor over at the high school, and let me tell you, no matter how much you scrub and bleach, you just never feel like a place is completely empty of them. Even the new science wing they built three years ago—it's supposed to be state-of-the-art, all glass and polished concrete. But I'm always looking over my shoulder when I mop those floors. \n\nThe floors in the cafeteria are the worst, though. The kids just drop everything. Tater tots, half-eaten apples, you name it. The other day, I found an entire unopened chocolate milk carton shoved behind a radiator. I don't know why they do that. The lunch ladies try their best, bless them. Mary, the head cook, she makes these amazing yeast rolls from scratch on Thursdays. The whole hallway smells like a bakery by 10 AM. She gave me her recipe once, but mine never turn out as fluffy. I think it's because my kitchen at home is too drafty. The dough doesn't rise right. I tried putting the bowl near the heating vent, but then my cat, Barnaby, knocked it over. He's a good cat, mostly, just a little clumsy. He's an orange tabby. I adopted him from the shelter after my husband left. They say orange cats share one brain cell, and honestly, with Barnaby, I believe it. He spends hours just staring at the washing machine...\n\nBut, um... what were you asking? Oh, about a clean place. Yeah, I don't think it matters how modern or clean it is. I'm just always expecting them to be there, hiding in the walls or something. I just can't turn that feeling off.\n\nDoctor: Would your fear of rats and mice decrease if you knew someone was with you to protect you when you encountered them?\n\nPatient: I don't think it would make a difference, honestly. Even if someone is right there next to me, the terror is just... it's too big. Like, my coworker Gary, he works the late shift with me sometimes. He's a really big guy, used to play defensive tackle in college, and he always tells me he'll handle it if we see anything. He's sweet like that. He actually just bought this massive new truck, a bright blue Ford. He's so proud of it, he parks it all the way at the back of the lot across two spaces so it doesn't get scratched. Which is silly, because the school lot is practically empty after 4 PM anyway. \n\nWell, except for the drama kids. They stay so late rehearsing. They're putting on The Music Man this spring, and the costumes are just darling. I saw them practicing with these little straw boater hats. I used to have a hat just like that. I wore it to a Fourth of July picnic years ago, back when my husband and I were still married. We went down to the river, but the whole day was kind of a disaster because the cooler leaked all over the trunk of the car and ruined the potato salad. I use my grandmother's recipe for potato salad, it has a little bit of dill pickle relish in it, which sounds weird, but it really gives it a nice tang. You have to use the name-brand mayonnaise though, the generic stuff just separates if it gets too warm...\n\nBut, um... gosh, I'm doing it again, aren't I? I'm sorry. You asked if having someone there helps. No, it really doesn't. Even with Gary standing right beside me holding a broom, if I even think I see a mouse, I still completely freeze up and panic. It doesn't matter who is standing there to protect me, the fear is exactly the same.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don't... I don't really know if that's even possible, honestly. A guaranteed free area? It sounds nice, but my mind just doesn't work that way. Even if they told me a room was completely sealed off, I'd still be checking the corners. It's like how they told us the new staff lounge was completely soundproof. They put in these special ceiling tiles and everything so we wouldn't hear the band practice down the hall. But you can still hear the tubas. It's this low, vibrating hum right around 2:30 every afternoon. 

The band director, Mr. Henderson, he's so intense about it. He makes those kids march out on the football field even when it's pouring rain. I feel bad for them, honestly. Their uniforms get completely soaked, and they have these heavy wool jackets with the gold braiding. I used to do alterations on the side, you know? Hemming pants, taking in dresses. I fixed a prom dress for the principal's daughter once. It was this gorgeous seafoam green satin, but it snagged so easily. I had to use the tiniest needle I owned. I keep all my sewing supplies in this old vintage cookie tin I found at a flea market. It has a picture of a windmill on it...

Oh, I'm sorry, I'm rambling again. I just... I don't know if a guaranteed safe area would help. I feel like I'd just be waiting for the guarantee to be broken, you know? I'd still be terrified.

But I'm always looking over my shoulder when I mop those floors. \n\nThe floors in the cafeteria are the worst, though. The kids just drop everything. Tater tots, half-eaten apples, you name it. The other day, I found an entire unopened chocolate milk carton shoved behind a radiator. I don't know why they do that. The lunch ladies try their best, bless them. Mary, the head cook, she makes these amazing yeast rolls from scratch on Thursdays. The whole hallway smells like a bakery by 10 AM. She gave me her recipe once, but mine never turn out as fluffy. I think it's because my kitchen at home is too drafty. The dough doesn't rise right. I tried putting the bowl near the heating vent, but then my cat, Barnaby, knocked it over. He's a good cat, mostly, just a little clumsy. He's an orange tabby. I adopted him from the shelter after my husband left. They say orange cats share one brain cell, and honestly, with Barnaby, I believe it. He spends hours just staring at the washing machine...\n\nBut, um... what were you asking? Oh, about a clean place. Yeah, I don't think it matters how modern or clean it is. I'm just always expecting them to be there, hiding in the walls or something. I just can't turn that feeling off.\n\nDoctor: Would your fear of rats and mice decrease if you knew someone was with you to protect you when you encountered them?\n\nPatient: I don't think it would make a difference, honestly. Even if someone is right there next to me, the terror is just... it's too big. Like, my coworker Gary, he works the late shift with me sometimes. He's a really big guy, used to play defensive tackle in college, and he always tells me he'll handle it if we see anything. He's sweet like that. He actually just bought this massive new truck, a bright blue Ford. He's so proud of it, he parks it all the way at the back of the lot across two spaces so it doesn't get scratched. Which is silly, because the school lot is practically empty after 4 PM anyway. \n\nWell, except for the drama kids. They stay so late rehearsing. They're putting on The Music Man this spring, and the costumes are just darling. I saw them practicing with these little straw boater hats. I used to have a hat just like that. I wore it to a Fourth of July picnic years ago, back when my husband and I were still married. We went down to the river, but the whole day was kind of a disaster because the cooler leaked all over the trunk of the car and ruined the potato salad. I use my grandmother's recipe for potato salad, it has a little bit of dill pickle relish in it, which sounds weird, but it really gives it a nice tang. You have to use the name-brand mayonnaise though, the generic stuff just separates if it gets too warm...\n\nBut, um... gosh, I'm doing it again, aren't I? I'm sorry. You asked if having someone there helps. No, it really doesn't. Even with Gary standing right beside me holding a broom, if I even think I see a mouse, I still completely freeze up and panic. It doesn't matter who is standing there to protect me, the fear is exactly the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of rats and mice would be less intense if you were in a similar work environment but with guaranteed rat- and mouse-free areas?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't really know if that's even possible, honestly. A guaranteed free area? It sounds nice, but my mind just doesn't work that way. Even if they told me a room was completely sealed off, I'd still be checking the corners. It's like how they told us the new staff lounge was completely soundproof. They put in these special ceiling tiles and everything so we wouldn't hear the band practice down the hall. But you can still hear the tubas. It's this low, vibrating hum right around 2:30 every afternoon. \n\nThe band director, Mr. Henderson, he's so intense about it. He makes those kids march out on the football field even when it's pouring rain. I feel bad for them, honestly. Their uniforms get completely soaked, and they have these heavy wool jackets with the gold braiding. I used to do alterations on the side, you know? Hemming pants, taking in dresses. I fixed a prom dress for the principal's daughter once. It was this gorgeous seafoam green satin, but it snagged so easily. I had to use the tiniest needle I owned. I keep all my sewing supplies in this old vintage cookie tin I found at a flea market. It has a picture of a windmill on it...\n\nOh, I'm sorry, I'm rambling again. I just... I don't know if a guaranteed safe area would help. I feel like I'd just be waiting for the guarantee to be broken, you know? I'd still be terrified."
